    import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';
    
    async function start(page) {
      await page.goto('/');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Start game' }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
    }
    
    async function drop(page, column, count) {
      const result = page.waitForResponse((response) => response.url().endsWith('/moves') && response.request().method() === 'POST');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: `Drop in column ${column + 1}`, exact: true }).click();
      expect((await result).status()).toBe(200);
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText(`${count} / 42`);
    }
    
    test('gravity, turn changes, highlight, refresh, and a new game', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      await drop(page, 3, 1);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '5');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Yellow’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await drop(page, 3, 2);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(1);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '4');
      const id = await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.getItem('connect4.gameId'));
      await page.reload();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('2 / 42');
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '4');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'New game' }).click();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('0 / 42');
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(0);
      expect(await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.getItem('connect4.gameId'))).not.toBe(id);
    });
    
    test('a full column is disabled', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) await drop(page, 2, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Column 3 is full' })).toBeDisabled();
      await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 4', exact: true })).toBeEnabled();
    });
    
    test('a win ends play and survives refresh', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      for (const [i, column] of [0,1,0,1,0,1,0].entries()) await drop(page, column, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red wins!' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.reload();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red wins!' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(1);
    });
    
    test('a full board without a winner is a draw', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      const moves = [6,6,6,5,2,2,5,1,4,3,3,6,3,4,0,4,1,0,4,4,1,6,5,1,0,5,1,4,3,1,5,3,0,6,5,2,2,0,2,3,2,0];
      for (const [i, column] of moves.entries()) await drop(page, column, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'It’s a draw.' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
    });
    
    test('a stale tab reloads without adding an unintended move', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      const other = await page.context().newPage();
      await other.goto('/');
      await expect(other.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('0 / 42');
      await drop(page, 0, 1);
      await other.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 2', exact: true }).click();
      await expect(other.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('The board changed.');
      await expect(other.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
      await expect(other.locator('[data-row="5"][data-column="1"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-player', '0');
      await drop(other, 1, 2);
    });
    
    test('a lost response is recovered without resending the move', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      await page.route('**/api/games/*/moves', async (route) => {
        const response = await route.fetch();
        expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
        await route.abort('failed');
      });
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 4', exact: true }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('could not confirm');
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.unroute('**/api/games/*/moves');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Reload game' }).click();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Yellow’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-column', '3');
    });
    
    test('the board fits a small screen and supports keyboard play', async ({ page }) => {
      await page.setViewportSize({ width: 360, height: 780 });
      await start(page);
      expect(await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= innerWidth)).toBe(true);
      const column = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 1', exact: true });
      await column.focus();
      await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
    });

        const pending = Object.keys({...manifest.dependencies, vite: manifest.devDependencies.vite}).map(n => 'node_modules/' + n);
        function resolve(name, from) {
         let base = from;
         while (true) {
          const candidate = path.join(base, 'node_modules', name);
          if (source.packages[candidate]) return candidate;
          if (!base || base === '.') break;
          base = path.dirname(base);
          if (base === '.') base = '';
         }
         throw new Error('Missing lock entry: ' + from + ' -> ' + name);
        }
        while (pending.length) {
         const key = pending.pop();
         if (packages[key]) continue;
         const entry = structuredClone(source.packages[key]);
         if (!entry) throw new Error(key);
         delete entry.peer;
         packages[key] = entry;
         for (const name of Object.keys({...entry.dependencies, ...entry.optionalDependencies})) pending.push(resolve(name, key));
        }

## Assistant

No—the environment didn’t block everything. Existing tools worked, but it prevented me from installing the missing ones.

| Missing | Needed for |
|---|---|
| Go | Running and testing the backend directly |
| Docker and Compose | Building images and running the app/database |
| `kubectl` and `kind` | Running and verifying local Kubernetes |
| PostgreSQL client (`psql`) | Optional database inspection |
| Frontend dependencies, including Vite and Playwright | Building React and running browser tests |

**Available:** Node.js 20.20.2, npm 10.8.2, Git, curl, Make, Python, and GCC. I could create files, run JavaScript tests, and perform static checks.

There were two installation barriers:

- **Terminal network access was disabled.** Downloads failed—for example, curl reported `Could not resolve host: go.dev`. Required npm packages also weren’t cached.
- **Administrator access was disabled.** `sudo` reported that the environment’s “no new privileges” restriction prevented elevation.

That doesn’t prove every possible installation method would fail, but it blocked the normal paths available here. Documentation browsing worked through a separate tool.

For the simplest startup, you only need **Docker with Compose** on your machine; Go, Node, and PostgreSQL are supplied by the container build. Add **`kubectl` and `kind`** for Kubernetes.

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    for tool in docker kind kubectl; do
      command -v "$tool" >/dev/null || { echo "Missing tool: $tool. See README.md." >&2; exit 1; }
    done
    docker info >/dev/null
    if ! kind get clusters | grep -qx connect4; then
      kind create cluster --name connect4 --config deploy/kind.yaml --wait 120s
    fi
    docker build -t connect4:local .
    kind load docker-image connect4:local --name connect4
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 apply -k deploy/k8s
    # Restart after loading the image, including when reusing the :local tag.
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout restart deployment/connect4
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status statefulset/postgres --timeout=180s
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status deployment/connect4 --timeout=180s
    echo 'Connect Four is ready at http://localhost:8080 (Ctrl+C stops forwarding).'
    exec kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 port-forward --address 127.0.0.1 service/connect4 8080:80
    apiVersion: v1
    kind: Service
    metadata:
      name: connect4
    spec:
      selector:
        app: connect4
      ports:
        - name: http
          port: 80
          targetPort: http
    ---
    apiVersion: apps/v1
    kind: Deployment
    metadata:
      name: connect4
    spec:
      replicas: 1
      strategy:
        type: Recreate
      selector:
        matchLabels:
          app: connect4
      template:
        metadata:
          labels:
            app: connect4
        spec:
          securityContext:
            runAsNonRoot: true
            runAsUser: 10001
            runAsGroup: 10001
            seccompProfile:
              type: RuntimeDefault
          containers:
            - name: app
              image: connect4:local
              imagePullPolicy: Never
              ports:
                - name: http
                  containerPort: 8080
              env:
                - name: DATABASE_URL
                  valueFrom:
                    secretKeyRef:
                      name: database
                      key: DATABASE_URL
              startupProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /healthz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 2
                failureThreshold: 60
              readinessProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /readyz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 5
              livenessProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /healthz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 10
              resources:
                requests:
                  cpu: 50m
                  memory: 32Mi
                limits:
                  cpu: "1"
                  memory: 128Mi
              securityContext:
                allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
                readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
                capabilities:
                  drop: ["ALL"]
    apiVersion: v1
    kind: Service
    metadata:
      name: postgres
    spec:
      clusterIP: None
      selector:
        app: postgres
      ports:
        - name: postgres
          port: 5432
          targetPort: 5432
    ---
    apiVersion: apps/v1
    kind: StatefulSet
    metadata:
      name: postgres
    spec:
      serviceName: postgres
      replicas: 1
      selector:
        matchLabels:
          app: postgres
      template:
        metadata:
          labels:
            app: postgres
        spec:
          securityContext:
            runAsUser: 999
            runAsGroup: 999
            fsGroup: 999
            runAsNonRoot: true
          containers:
            - name: postgres
              image: postgres:18-bookworm
              ports:
                - containerPort: 5432
              envFrom:
                - secretRef:
                    name: database
              volumeMounts:
                - name: data
                  mountPath: /var/lib/postgresql
              startupProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 2
                failureThreshold: 60
              readinessProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 5
              livenessProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 10
                failureThreshold: 6
              resources:
                requests:
                  cpu: 100m
                  memory: 128Mi
                limits:
                  cpu: "1"
                  memory: 512Mi
              securityContext:
                allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
                capabilities:
                  drop: ["ALL"]
      volumeClaimTemplates:
        - metadata:
            name: data
          spec:
            accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]
            resources:
              requests:
                storage: 1Gi
    apiVersion: kustomize.config.k8s.io/v1beta1
    kind: Kustomization
    namespace: connect4
    resources:
      - namespace.yaml
      - secret.yaml
      - postgres.yaml
      - app.yaml
    kind: Cluster
    apiVersion: kind.x-k8s.io/v1alpha4
    nodes:
      - role: control-plane
    .PHONY: up down dev-db backend frontend test test-integration test-container e2e verify k8s-up k8s-down k8s-reset fmt
    
    up:
        docker compose up --build --wait --wait-timeout 120
    
    down:
        docker compose down
    
    dev-db:
        docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d --wait db
    
    backend:
        go mod download
        DATABASE_URL="$${DATABASE_URL:-postgres://connect4:connect4_local@127.0.0.1:5432/connect4?sslmode=disable}" go run ./cmd/server
    
    frontend:
        npm --prefix web ci
        npm --prefix web run dev
    
    test:
        go vet ./...
        go test -race -count=1 ./...
        npm --prefix web test
    
    test-integration:
        @test -n "$${TEST_DATABASE_URL}" || (echo 'Set TEST_DATABASE_URL to a dedicated test database.' >&2; exit 1)
        go test -race -count=1 -v ./internal/store
    
    test-container:
        bash scripts/test-container.sh
    
    e2e:
        npm --prefix web run test:e2e
    
    verify:
        node scripts/verify.mjs
    
    k8s-up:
        bash scripts/k8s-up.sh
    
    k8s-down:
        kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 scale deployment/connect4 statefulset/postgres --replicas=0
    
    k8s-reset:
        kind delete cluster --name connect4
    
    fmt:
        gofmt -w cmd internal

## Assistant

Verify that Kubernetes starts both services and preserves a game after replacing their pods.

1. **Install prerequisites**, if needed: [kubectl](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/tools/install-kubectl-linux/) and [kind](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/). Kind runs a local Kubernetes cluster using Docker.

2. **Start Kubernetes** from the project directory:

   ```bash
   sudo docker compose down
   sudo make k8s-up
   ```

   Leave this terminal open—it forwards port 8080 to the app. Use `sudo` consistently below so the commands use the same Kubernetes configuration.

3. **Check the workloads** in a second terminal:

   ```bash
   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 get pods,pvc
   ```

   Expect:
   - One `connect4-…` pod and one `postgres-0` pod, both `1/1 Running`.
   - The PostgreSQL storage claim showing `Bound`.

4. **Create a test game.** Open **http://localhost:8080**, start a new game, and play several moves. Remember the board, current turn, and highlighted disc. Kubernetes uses a separate database from Compose.

5. **Restart the database and app.** These commands replace their running pods. [Kubernetes restart reference](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/generated/kubectl_rollout/kubectl_rollout_restart/)

   ```bash
   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout restart statefulset/postgres
   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status statefulset/postgres --timeout=180s

   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout restart deployment/connect4
   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status deployment/connect4 --timeout=180s
   ```

6. **Restore browser access.** The app restart interrupts port forwarding. In the first terminal, stop any remaining forwarding process with Ctrl+C, then run:

   ```bash
   sudo kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 port-forward --address 127.0.0.1 service/connect4 8080:80
   ```

   Refresh the **same browser tab**. The board, turn, and last-disc highlight should match. Play another move to confirm the game continues.

If those checks succeed, Kubernetes startup and persistence across pod replacement are verified.

Install `kind` using its official Linux x86-64 binary. [Official instructions](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/)

```bash
(
  set -e
  kind_download_dir=$(mktemp -d)
  trap 'rm -rf "$kind_download_dir"' EXIT

  curl -fsSL https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/dl/v0.33.0/kind-linux-amd64 \
    -o "$kind_download_dir/kind"

  sudo install -m 0755 "$kind_download_dir/kind" /usr/local/bin/kind
)
```

Verify both:

```bash
kubectl version --client
kind version
```

Then start the project in Kubernetes:

```bash
cd /home/yellowrys/project/connect4
sudo docker compose down
sudo make k8s-up
```

Leave that terminal open once it starts forwarding port 8080.

## Assistant

This is a [known Snap Docker issue](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/known-issues/#docker-installed-with-snap): Docker cannot access kind’s temporary archive under `/tmp`.

I fixed the [startup script](/home/yellowrys/project/connect4/scripts/k8s-up.sh) to use a temporary directory inside the project and clean it afterward. Shell checks and simulated success/failure checks passed.

Retry:

```bash
sudo make k8s-up
```

It will reuse the existing cluster. The fix still needs verification with Docker on your machine.

      "name": "connect-four-web",

      }

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    compose=(docker compose -p connect4-tests -f compose.test.yaml)
    cleanup() { "${compose[@]}" down --volumes; }
    trap cleanup EXIT
    "${compose[@]}" up --build --abort-on-container-exit --exit-code-from tests
    # A separate project and disposable database, independent of saved player games.
    services:
      db:
        image: postgres:18-bookworm
        environment:
          POSTGRES_USER: connect4
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: connect4_test
          POSTGRES_DB: connect4_test
        healthcheck:
          test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U connect4 -d connect4_test"]
          interval: 2s
          timeout: 3s
          retries: 20
        tmpfs:
          - /var/lib/postgresql

        build:
          context: .
          target: backend
        environment:
          TEST_DATABASE_URL: postgres://connect4:connect4_test@db:5432/connect4_test?sslmode=disable
        depends_on:
          db:
            condition: service_healthy
        command: ["go", "test", "-race", "-count=1", "-v", "./..."]

## Assistant

Run these in your normal terminal. If Kubernetes is still forwarding port 8080, stop that forwarding with **Ctrl+C** first.

```bash
cd /home/yellowrys/project/connect4

# Backend tests, including PostgreSQL integration and race detection
sudo make test-container

# Rebuild and start the updated app
sudo docker compose up --build --wait --wait-timeout 120

# Startup and API smoke checks
make verify

# Install the current frontend dependencies and run browser tests
npm --prefix web ci
make e2e
```

The Docker build also runs `go vet`, backend unit tests, JavaScript API tests, and the frontend production build.

If Playwright reports a missing browser:

```bash
cd web
npx playwright install --with-deps chromium
cd ..
make e2e
```

If you also changed Kubernetes configuration or startup scripts:

```bash
sudo docker compose down
sudo make k8s-up
```

Leave that terminal open, then run `make verify` and `make e2e` in another terminal against the Kubernetes app.

    .players { display: flex; gap: 18px; font-size: 12px; font-weight: 600; }
    .players > span { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 6px; }
    .legend-disc { width: 22px; height: 22px; display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; border-radius: 50%; font-style: normal; font-size: 11px; }
    .primary-button { display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 23px; border: 1px solid var(--blue); border-radius: 8px; padding: 11px 16px; background: var(--blue); color: #fff; font-size: 12px; font-weight: 600; transition: background 150ms; }
    .primary-button:hover:enabled { background: #172f65; }
    .primary-button:disabled { opacity: .55; }
    .primary-button > span { font-size: 17px; line-height: 1; }
    .page-footer { text-align: center; color: #747974; font-size: 11px; margin-top: 24px; line-height: 1.6; }
    .page-footer p { margin: 2px 0; }
    .error-message { margin-top: 22px; padding: 12px 15px; border-radius: 8px; background: #fff0e9; color: #893528; font-size: 13px; line-height: 1.5; }
    .error-message p { margin: 0; }
    .text-button { color: #7b2e21; margin-top: 7px; padding: 3px 0; border: 0; background: transparent; font-size: 13px; font-weight: 700; text-decoration: underline; }
    .storage-notice { color: #775923; font-size: 12px; line-height: 1.5; margin-bottom: 0; }
    @keyframes drop-in { from { transform: translateY(-18px); opacity: .5; } to { transform: translateY(0); opacity: 1; } }
    @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation: none !important; transition: none !important; } }
    @media (max-width: 600px) {
      .app { padding: 24px 16px; }
      .page-header { margin-bottom: 23px; }
      .brand { font-size: 22px; }
      .device-label { font-size: 10px; }
      .game-card { padding: 25px 18px 23px; border-radius: 16px; }
      .status-bar { padding: 12px; gap: 9px; margin-top: 22px; }
      .status-bar h2 { font-size: 15px; }
      .status-bar p { font-size: 11px; }
      .move-count { display: none; }
      .board { gap: 6px; padding: 12px 10px 14px; border-radius: 11px; }
      .column { gap: 7px; }
      .column-labels { padding: 0 10px; }
      .cell.last-disc { outline-width: 2px; outline-offset: -4px; }
      .game-footer { margin-top: 24px; }
      .players { gap: 12px; font-size: 11px; }
      .primary-button { gap: 14px; padding: 10px 12px; }
    }

              <span>Connect <strong>four.</strong></span>
            </a>
          </header>
    
          <section className="game-card" aria-label="Connect Four game">
            <div className="game-heading">
              <h1>Play Connect 4.</h1>
            </div>
    
            <div className="status-bar" role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">
              <span className={`turn-disc player-${player}`} aria-hidden="true">{player || '4'}</span>
              <div><h2>{title}</h2><p>{subtitle}</p></div>

            <div className="board-shell">
              <div className="board" role="group" aria-label="Game board: 6 rows and 7 columns" aria-busy={busy}>
                {Array.from({ length: 7 }, (_, column) => {
                  const full = Boolean(game && game.board[0][column] !== 0);
                  const disabled = busy || needsReload || !playing || !game.availableColumns.includes(column);
                  return (
                    <button key={column} type="button" className="column" disabled={disabled}
                      aria-label={full ? `Column ${column + 1} is full` : `Drop in column ${column + 1}`}
                      onClick={() => drop(column)}>
                      {(game?.board || emptyBoard).map((row, rowIndex) => {
                        const last = game?.lastMove?.row === rowIndex && game?.lastMove?.column === column;
                        return <span key={`${rowIndex}-${row[column]}-${last}`} className={`cell player-${row[column]}${last ? ' last-disc' : ''}`}
                          data-row={rowIndex} data-column={column} data-player={row[column]} data-last={last ? 'true' : undefined}
                          role="img" aria-label={`Row ${rowIndex + 1}, column ${column + 1}: ${row[column] ? names[row[column]] : 'empty'}${last ? ', last move' : ''}`}>
                          {row[column] > 0 && <span aria-hidden="true">{row[column]}</span>}
                        </span>;
                      })}
                    </button>
                  );
                })}
              </div>
              <div className="board-foot" aria-hidden="true" />
            </div>
    
            {error && <div className="error-message" role="alert"><p>{error}</p>{needsReload && <button className="text-button" onClick={reload} disabled={busy}>Reload game</button>}</div>}

    import { test, expect } from '@playwright/test';
    
    async function start(page) {
      await page.goto('/');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Start game' }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
    }
    
    async function drop(page, column, count) {
      const result = page.waitForResponse((response) => response.url().endsWith('/moves') && response.request().method() === 'POST');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: `Drop in column ${column + 1}`, exact: true }).click();
      expect((await result).status()).toBe(200);
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText(`${count} / 42`);
    }
    
    test('gravity, turn changes, highlight, refresh, and a new game', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      await drop(page, 3, 1);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '5');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Yellow’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await drop(page, 3, 2);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(1);
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '4');
      const id = await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.getItem('connect4.gameId'));
      await page.reload();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('2 / 42');
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-row', '4');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'New game' }).click();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('0 / 42');
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(0);
      expect(await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.getItem('connect4.gameId'))).not.toBe(id);
    });
    
    test('a full column is disabled', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      for (let i = 0; i < 6; i++) await drop(page, 2, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Column 3 is full' })).toBeDisabled();
      await expect(page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 4', exact: true })).toBeEnabled();
    });
    
    test('a win ends play and survives refresh', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      for (const [i, column] of [0,1,0,1,0,1,0].entries()) await drop(page, column, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red wins!' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.reload();
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Red wins!' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveCount(1);
    });
    
    test('a full board without a winner is a draw', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      const moves = [6,6,6,5,2,2,5,1,4,3,3,6,3,4,0,4,1,0,4,4,1,6,5,1,0,5,1,4,3,1,5,3,0,6,5,2,2,0,2,3,2,0];
      for (const [i, column] of moves.entries()) await drop(page, column, i + 1);
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'It’s a draw.' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
    });
    
    test('a stale tab reloads without adding an unintended move', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      const other = await page.context().newPage();
      await other.goto('/');
      await expect(other.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('0 / 42');
      await drop(page, 0, 1);
      await other.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 2', exact: true }).click();
      await expect(other.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('The board changed.');
      await expect(other.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
      await expect(other.locator('[data-row="5"][data-column="1"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-player', '0');
      await drop(other, 1, 2);
    });
    
    test('a lost response is recovered without resending the move', async ({ page }) => {
      await start(page);
      await page.route('**/api/games/*/moves', async (route) => {
        const response = await route.fetch();
        expect(response.status()).toBe(200);
        await route.abort('failed');
      });
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 4', exact: true }).click();
      await expect(page.getByRole('alert')).toContainText('could not confirm');
      await expect(page.locator('.column:enabled')).toHaveCount(0);
      await page.unroute('**/api/games/*/moves');
      await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Reload game' }).click();
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
      await expect(page.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Yellow’s turn' })).toBeVisible();
      await expect(page.locator('[data-last="true"]')).toHaveAttribute('data-column', '3');
    });
    
    test('the board fits a small screen and supports keyboard play', async ({ page }) => {
      await page.setViewportSize({ width: 360, height: 780 });
      await start(page);
      expect(await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= innerWidth)).toBe(true);
      const column = page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Drop in column 1', exact: true });
      await column.focus();
      await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
      await expect(page.locator('.move-count')).toHaveText('1 / 42');
    });
    import { defineConfig, devices } from '@playwright/test';

      testDir: './e2e',

        trace: 'retain-on-failure',
        screenshot: 'only-on-failure',
      },
      projects: [{ name: 'chromium', use: { ...devices['Desktop Chrome'] } }],
    });

## Activity

    $ cat README.md Makefile Dockerfile compose.yaml compose.dev.yaml compose.test.yaml .gitignore
    # Connect Four
    
    A local two-player Connect Four game. React renders the board; Go validates every
    move; PostgreSQL stores the ordered history. Both players share one browser on one
    device. Red starts each game.
    
    ## Run locally
    
    Install **Docker Engine with the Compose plugin**, or **Docker Desktop** with WSL
    integration enabled if using Windows/WSL. Docker must be running.
    
    - [Docker Engine for Ubuntu](https://docs.docker.com/engine/install/ubuntu/)
    - [Docker Desktop for Windows / WSL](https://docs.docker.com/desktop/features/wsl/)
    
    From the project directory:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose up --build
    ```
    
    Open **http://localhost:8080**. The image builds the frontend and backend, runs the
    backend unit tests, and starts alongside PostgreSQL. The server waits for the
    database and automatically applies versioned SQL migrations. You do not need Go,
    Node, or PostgreSQL installed on the host for this path.
    
    Click **Start game**, then take turns choosing columns. Filled columns are
    disabled. Connect four discs horizontally, vertically, or diagonally to win. A
    full board without a winner is a draw. The outlined disc is the most recent move.
    **New game** creates a fresh game ID with red starting again.
    
    The browser remembers the active game ID in `localStorage`. Refreshing reloads
    the game from PostgreSQL, including the last-disc highlight and any result.
    PostgreSQL uses a named Docker volume, so ordinary stops and restarts preserve
    games. Clearing browser storage loses the browser's reference to the active game.
    
    Stop containers, preserving games:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose down
    ```
    
    Start in the background and wait for readiness, if Make is installed:
    
    ```bash
    make up
    ```
    
    **Erase all saved games deliberately:** `docker compose down --volumes`.
    
    Port 8080 is bound to the local machine. PostgreSQL is internal to the Compose
    network. The included credentials are for local development.
    
    ## Develop with Go and React
    
    Install [Go 1.23 or newer](https://go.dev/doc/install) (the container uses Go 1.26),
    [Node.js 22 LTS](https://nodejs.org/) (or Node >=20.19), and Make. The Go HTTP server
    uses the standard library; `pgx` supplies PostgreSQL connections. Vite builds and
    serves React during development. No Go framework or ORM is involved.
    
    Start PostgreSQL with its development-only port exposed:
    
    ```bash
    make dev-db
    ```
    
    In a second terminal, install Go dependencies and start the API:
    
    ```bash
    make backend
    ```
    
    In a third terminal, install frontend dependencies and start Vite:
    
    ```bash
    make frontend
    ```
    
    Open **http://localhost:5173**. Vite forwards `/api` requests to Go on port 8080;
    frontend edits update automatically. Restart `make backend` after Go changes.
    The Go server's root page needs a frontend build (`npm --prefix web run build`),
    but its API works independently during development.
    
    Stop the development database with:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml down
    ```
    
    Environment variables:
    
    | Name | Meaning | Default |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `DATABASE_URL` | PostgreSQL connection string | Required; `make backend` supplies a local value |
    | `ADDR` | Go listen address | `:8080` |
    | `STATIC_DIR` | Built frontend directory | `web/dist` |
    | `TEST_DATABASE_URL` | Dedicated PostgreSQL database for integration tests | Unset; integration tests skip |
    | `BASE_URL` | Running application URL for smoke/browser tests | `http://localhost:8080` |
    
    ## Verify and test
    
    With Node available and the application running, verify startup and an actual
    game against the database:
    
    ```bash
    make verify
    ```
    
    This checks readiness, served HTML, legal moves, a win, retrieval of saved state,
    and rejection of further moves. It creates one small test game in the database.
    
    Run backend unit tests and PostgreSQL integration tests in containers, using a
    separate temporary database that is removed afterward:
    
    ```bash
    make test-container
    ```
    
    For native development, after `go mod download`:
    
    ```bash
    make test
    ```
    
    This runs `go vet`, Go tests with the race detector, and the dependency-free
    JavaScript API tests. PostgreSQL tests skip unless `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set.
    The race detector requires a C compiler when running natively on Linux; the Go
    container includes one. To explicitly require integration tests against your own
    dedicated test database:
    
    ```bash
    TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgres://user:password@localhost:5432/connect4_test?sslmode=disable' make test-integration
    ```
    
    Browser tests require the full application running at `BASE_URL`:
    
    ```bash
    cd web
    npm ci
    npx playwright install --with-deps chromium
    npm run test:e2e
    ```
    
    They cover turn changes, gravity, last-disc highlighting, refresh, new games,
    full columns, wins, draws, stale tabs, a lost response after a committed move,
    keyboard controls, and a small screen. Go tests additionally cover both diagonal
    directions, yellow wins, invalid requests, rejection after game end, a win on the
    42nd move, migration reruns, and simultaneous requests.
    
    ## Kubernetes
    
    Install [kubectl](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/tools/) and
    [kind](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/) alongside Docker. Then:
    
    ```bash
    make k8s-up
    ```
    
    This creates a local `connect4` cluster if necessary, builds and loads the app
    image, applies `deploy/k8s`, waits for readiness, and forwards
    **http://localhost:8080**. Stop Compose first if it already uses that port. Ctrl+C
    stops forwarding; the workloads keep running.
    
    The startup script puts kind's temporary files in a unique directory inside the
    project so Snap-installed Docker can access the image archive. It removes that
    directory after loading, including if loading fails.
    
    There is one app replica and one PostgreSQL replica. A **Deployment** manages the
    app process; a **StatefulSet** gives PostgreSQL a stable identity and a
    **PersistentVolumeClaim** requests disk storage. Restarting pods preserves data.
    Liveness checks detect a stopped process; readiness checks determine whether the
    app can reach PostgreSQL. All helper commands explicitly select the local kind
    context.
    
    Stop the workloads while keeping their data with `make k8s-down`. Running
    `make k8s-up` starts them again. **`make k8s-reset` deletes the local cluster and
    its saved games.** Compose and Kubernetes use separate databases.
    
    The manifests target a local kind cluster, whose default storage provisioner
    supplies the disk. For another cluster, provide a default StorageClass, publish
    the app image to your registry, and update `image` / `imagePullPolicy` in
    `deploy/k8s/app.yaml`. Kubernetes Secrets supply database configuration.
    
    ## How it works
    
    ```text
    Browser (React + remembered game ID)
                     │ HTTP / JSON
                     ▼
    Go server (serves frontend + validates moves)
                     │ SQL
                     ▼
    PostgreSQL (games + ordered moves)
    ```
    
    | Request | Result |
    | --- | --- |
    | `POST /api/games` | `201`: a new game and its random ID |
    | `GET /api/games/{id}` | `200`: reconstructed state, or `404` |
    | `POST /api/games/{id}/moves` | `200`: updated state, or a validation error |
    | `GET /healthz` | Process is responding |
    | `GET /readyz` | Process can reach PostgreSQL |
    
    A move body looks like:
    
    ```json
    { "column": 3, "expectedMoveCount": 8 }
    ```
    
    API columns are numbered **0–6**; the interface labels them **1–7**. Board row zero
    is the top. `board` cells are `0` (empty), `1` (red), or `2` (yellow).
    `status` is `playing`, `won`, or `draw`. `nextPlayer` is zero after completion;
    `winner` is zero unless someone won. `lastMove` is null before the first move,
    otherwise it contains `row`, `column`, and `player`. `availableColumns` is empty
    after completion. A new state includes `id` and `moveCount` as well.
    
    For each move, Go starts a **transaction**: a group of SQL operations that all
    succeed or all roll back. It locks that game's row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`,
    reads the moves in order, reconstructs the board, compares `expectedMoveCount`,
    checks legality, and appends the new move. Another request for the same game waits
    and then sees the committed history. An outdated request receives
    `409 stale_state`, so a duplicate click cannot silently become the next player's
    turn. The client refreshes the state after a conflict. If a response is lost, it
    requires a reload before allowing another move rather than resending the request.
    
    There is no separate stored board or result to get out of sync with the moves.
    Win detection runs before the full-board draw check, including on move 42.
    
    Main files:
    
    - `internal/game`: pure game rules and their unit tests.
    - `internal/store`: PostgreSQL transactions, migrations, integration tests.
    - `internal/httpapi`: JSON validation, HTTP endpoints, static file serving.
    - `cmd/server`: configuration, startup, and graceful shutdown.
    - `web/src`: React interface, styling, and API client.
    - `web/e2e`: browser tests against the running app.
    - `deploy`: local kind configuration and Kubernetes resources.
    
    ## Troubleshooting
    
    - **Cannot connect to Docker:** start Docker / Docker Desktop. In WSL, enable
      integration for this distribution. Installing the CLI alone does not start an
      engine.
    - **Port already allocated:** stop the other app or change the local port mapping
      in `compose.yaml`; use the matching `BASE_URL` for verification.
    - **kind image loading reports `invalid output path` under `/tmp`:** Snap Docker
      has a different view of temporary directories. The startup script supplies a
      shared project-local `TMPDIR` for kind; rerun `sudo make k8s-up` with the updated
      script. It reuses the existing cluster. See
      [kind's Snap Docker guidance](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/known-issues/#docker-installed-with-snap).
    - **Database not ready:** inspect `docker compose logs db app`. Initial startup
      creates the database and runs migrations automatically.
    - **Cannot download tools, packages, or images:** initial setup requires external
      network access. Offline builds require previously cached dependencies and images.
    - **Reload game message:** the previous move may already be saved. Reload to
      retrieve the authoritative state before continuing.
    
    ## Verification in the build environment
    
    The initial implementation environment had Node.js 20.20.2 and npm 10.8.2, but no
    Go, Docker, Kubernetes tools, or PostgreSQL. External shell downloads failed with
    DNS resolution errors, no npm dependencies were cached, and administrator access
    was disabled. The JavaScript API tests ran successfully, along with JSX syntax,
    dependency lockfile, YAML, and shell syntax checks.
    
    Docker and Compose were subsequently installed and verified on the host machine.
    The developer confirmed that the game works at http://localhost:8080. Docker
    startup and browser operation are therefore confirmed on the host; this agent
    session still cannot access Docker because of its sandbox restrictions.
    
    The developer also confirmed that `sudo make test-container` passed on the host.
    This runs the Go tests, including the PostgreSQL integration tests, with the race
    detector against a separate temporary database.
    
    The developer confirmed that all seven Playwright browser tests passed on the
    host, covering game interactions, refresh recovery, full columns, wins, draws,
    stale tabs, lost responses, and keyboard play on a small screen.
    
    The developer confirmed successful Kubernetes startup and game persistence
    across replacement of the PostgreSQL and application pods on the host. This
    completes the planned first-iteration verification: Docker startup, backend and
    PostgreSQL tests, all seven browser tests, and Kubernetes startup and persistence.
    
    `node scripts/verify.mjs` remains available as a separate smoke check against a
    running application; successful execution of that standalone script has not been
    reported.
    .PHONY: up down dev-db backend frontend test test-integration test-container e2e verify k8s-up k8s-down k8s-reset fmt
    
    up:
        docker compose up --build --wait --wait-timeout 120
    
    down:
        docker compose down
    
    dev-db:
        docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml up -d --wait db
    
    backend:
        go mod download
        DATABASE_URL="$${DATABASE_URL:-postgres://connect4:connect4_local@127.0.0.1:5432/connect4?sslmode=disable}" go run ./cmd/server
    
    frontend:
        npm --prefix web ci
        npm --prefix web run dev
    
    test:
        go vet ./...
        go test -race -count=1 ./...
        npm --prefix web test
    
    test-integration:
        @test -n "$${TEST_DATABASE_URL}" || (echo 'Set TEST_DATABASE_URL to a dedicated test database.' >&2; exit 1)
        go test -race -count=1 -v ./internal/store
    
    test-container:
        bash scripts/test-container.sh
    
    e2e:
        npm --prefix web run test:e2e
    
    verify:
        node scripts/verify.mjs
    
    k8s-up:
        bash scripts/k8s-up.sh
    
    k8s-down:
        kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 scale deployment/connect4 statefulset/postgres --replicas=0
    
    k8s-reset:
        kind delete cluster --name connect4
    
    fmt:
        gofmt -w cmd internal
    FROM node:22-bookworm-slim AS frontend
    WORKDIR /src/web
    COPY web/package.json web/package-lock.json ./
    RUN npm ci
    COPY web/ ./
    RUN npm test && npm run build
    
    FROM golang:1.26-bookworm AS backend
    WORKDIR /src
    COPY go.mod go.sum ./
    RUN go mod download
    COPY cmd/ ./cmd/
    COPY internal/ ./internal/
    
    FROM backend AS test
    RUN go vet ./... && go test -race -count=1 ./...
    
    FROM test AS build
    RUN CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -trimpath -ldflags="-s -w" -o /out/connect4 ./cmd/server
    
    FROM scratch AS app
    WORKDIR /app
    COPY --from=build /out/connect4 ./connect4
    COPY --from=build /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt /etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt
    COPY --from=frontend /src/web/dist ./web/dist
    USER 10001:10001
    EXPOSE 8080
    HEALTHCHECK --interval=10s --timeout=4s --start-period=75s --retries=3 CMD ["/app/connect4", "healthcheck"]
    ENTRYPOINT ["/app/connect4"]
    services:
      app:
        build: .
        ports:
          - "127.0.0.1:8080:8080"
        environment:
          DATABASE_URL: postgres://connect4:connect4_local@db:5432/connect4?sslmode=disable
        depends_on:
          db:
            condition: service_healthy
        restart: unless-stopped
    
      db:
        image: postgres:18-bookworm
        environment:
          POSTGRES_USER: connect4
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: connect4_local
          POSTGRES_DB: connect4
        volumes:
          - postgres-data:/var/lib/postgresql
        healthcheck:
          test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U connect4 -d connect4"]
          interval: 2s
          timeout: 3s
          retries: 20
        restart: unless-stopped
    
    volumes:
      postgres-data:
    # Use alongside compose.yaml to expose PostgreSQL for native Go development.
    services:
      db:
        ports:
          - "127.0.0.1:5432:5432"
    # A separate project and disposable database, independent of saved player games.
    services:
      db:
        image: postgres:18-bookworm
        environment:
          POSTGRES_USER: connect4
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: connect4_test
          POSTGRES_DB: connect4_test
        healthcheck:
          test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U connect4 -d connect4_test"]
          interval: 2s
          timeout: 3s
          retries: 20
        tmpfs:
          - /var/lib/postgresql
      tests:
        build:
          context: .
          target: backend
        environment:
          TEST_DATABASE_URL: postgres://connect4:connect4_test@db:5432/connect4_test?sslmode=disable
        depends_on:
          db:
            condition: service_healthy
        command: ["go", "test", "-race", "-count=1", "-v", "./..."]

    .env
    bin/
    web/node_modules/

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    for tool in docker kind kubectl; do
      command -v "$tool" >/dev/null || { echo "Missing tool: $tool. See README.md." >&2; exit 1; }
    done
    docker info >/dev/null
    # Snap Docker cannot see kind's normal /tmp. Keep archives beside the project,
    # where both tools can access them, and remove them even if image loading fails.
    kind_temp_dir="$(mktemp -d "$PWD/kind-tmp.XXXXXX")"
    trap 'rm -rf "$kind_temp_dir"' EXIT
    if ! kind get clusters | grep -qx connect4; then
      TMPDIR="$kind_temp_dir" kind create cluster --name connect4 --config deploy/kind.yaml --wait 120s
    fi
    docker build -t connect4:local .
    TMPDIR="$kind_temp_dir" kind load docker-image connect4:local --name connect4
    rm -rf "$kind_temp_dir"
    trap - EXIT
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 apply -k deploy/k8s
    # Restart after loading the image, including when reusing the :local tag.
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout restart deployment/connect4
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status statefulset/postgres --timeout=180s
    kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 rollout status deployment/connect4 --timeout=180s
    echo 'Connect Four is ready at http://localhost:8080 (Ctrl+C stops forwarding).'
    exec kubectl --context kind-connect4 -n connect4 port-forward --address 127.0.0.1 service/connect4 8080:80
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    compose=(docker compose -p connect4-tests -f compose.test.yaml)
    cleanup() { "${compose[@]}" down --volumes; }
    trap cleanup EXIT
    "${compose[@]}" up --build --abort-on-container-exit --exit-code-from tests
    import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
    
    const base = (process.env.BASE_URL || 'http://localhost:8080').replace(/\/$/, '');
    async function call(path, options = {}, status = 200) {
      const response = await fetch(`${base}${path}`, {
        ...options,
        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
      });
      assert.equal(response.status, status, `${options.method || 'GET'} ${path}`);
      return response.json();
    }
    try {
      await call('/readyz');
      const page = await fetch(base, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000) });
      assert.equal(page.status, 200, 'frontend serves');
      assert.match(await page.text(), /Connect Four/);
      let game = await call('/api/games', { method: 'POST' }, 201);
      const path = `/api/games/${game.id}`;
      for (const column of [0,1,0,1,0,1,0]) {
        game = await call(`${path}/moves`, { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify({ column, expectedMoveCount: game.moveCount }) });
      }
      assert.equal(game.status, 'won');
      assert.equal(game.winner, 1);
      assert.deepEqual(await call(path), game, 'saved board matches after retrieval');
      const ended = await call(`${path}/moves`, { method: 'POST', body: JSON.stringify({ column: 2, expectedMoveCount: 7 }) }, 409);
      assert.equal(ended.code, 'game_finished');
      console.log(`Startup verified at ${base}: frontend, database, legal play, win, saved state, and rejection after game end.`);
    } catch (error) {
      console.error(`Startup verification failed at ${base}: ${error.message}`);
      process.exitCode = 1;
    }

    export default defineConfig({
      testDir: './e2e',

      workers: 1,
      reporter: 'list',

    apiVersion: v1
    kind: Service
    metadata:
      name: connect4
    spec:
      selector:
        app: connect4
      ports:
        - name: http
          port: 80
          targetPort: http
    ---
    apiVersion: apps/v1
    kind: Deployment
    metadata:
      name: connect4
    spec:
      replicas: 1
      strategy:
        type: Recreate
      selector:
        matchLabels:
          app: connect4
      template:
        metadata:
          labels:
            app: connect4
        spec:
          securityContext:
            runAsNonRoot: true
            runAsUser: 10001
            runAsGroup: 10001
            seccompProfile:
              type: RuntimeDefault
          containers:
            - name: app
              image: connect4:local
              imagePullPolicy: Never
              ports:
                - name: http
                  containerPort: 8080
              env:
                - name: DATABASE_URL
                  valueFrom:
                    secretKeyRef:
                      name: database
                      key: DATABASE_URL
              startupProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /healthz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 2
                failureThreshold: 60
              readinessProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /readyz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 5
              livenessProbe:
                httpGet:
                  path: /healthz
                  port: http
                periodSeconds: 10
              resources:
                requests:
                  cpu: 50m
                  memory: 32Mi
                limits:
                  cpu: "1"
                  memory: 128Mi
              securityContext:
                allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
                readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
                capabilities:
                  drop: ["ALL"]
    apiVersion: v1
    kind: Service
    metadata:
      name: postgres
    spec:
      clusterIP: None
      selector:
        app: postgres
      ports:
        - name: postgres
          port: 5432
          targetPort: 5432
    ---
    apiVersion: apps/v1
    kind: StatefulSet
    metadata:
      name: postgres
    spec:
      serviceName: postgres
      replicas: 1
      selector:
        matchLabels:
          app: postgres
      template:
        metadata:
          labels:
            app: postgres
        spec:
          securityContext:
            runAsUser: 999
            runAsGroup: 999
            fsGroup: 999
            runAsNonRoot: true
          containers:
            - name: postgres
              image: postgres:18-bookworm
              ports:
                - containerPort: 5432
              envFrom:
                - secretRef:
                    name: database
              volumeMounts:
                - name: data
                  mountPath: /var/lib/postgresql
              startupProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 2
                failureThreshold: 60
              readinessProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 5
              livenessProbe:
                exec:
                  command: ["pg_isready", "-U", "connect4", "-d", "connect4"]
                periodSeconds: 10
                failureThreshold: 6
              resources:
                requests:
                  cpu: 100m
                  memory: 128Mi
                limits:
                  cpu: "1"
                  memory: 512Mi
              securityContext:
                allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
                capabilities:
                  drop: ["ALL"]
      volumeClaimTemplates:
        - metadata:
            name: data
          spec:
            accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]
            resources:
              requests:
                storage: 1Gi
    # Development credentials for the local kind cluster.

    module connect4
    
    go 1.23.0
    
    require github.com/jackc/pgx/v5 v5.7.6
    
    require (
        github.com/jackc/pgpassfile v1.0.0 // indirect
        github.com/jackc/pgservicefile v0.0.0-20240606120523-5a60cdf6a761 // indirect
        github.com/jackc/puddle/v2 v2.2.2 // indirect
        golang.org/x/crypto v0.37.0 // indirect
        golang.org/x/sync v0.13.0 // indirect
        golang.org/x/text v0.24.0 // indirect

    package main
    
    import (
        "context"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "log/slog"
        "net/http"
        "os"
        "os/signal"
        "syscall"
        "time"
    
        "connect4/internal/httpapi"
        "connect4/internal/store"
    )
    
    func main() {
        logger := slog.New(slog.NewJSONHandler(os.Stdout, nil))
        if err := run(logger); err != nil { logger.Error("server stopped", "error", err); os.Exit(1) }
    }
    
    func run(logger *slog.Logger) error {
        // The container health check uses the application binary, so the final image
        // needs neither a shell nor curl.
        if len(os.Args) == 2 && os.Args[1] == "healthcheck" {
            client := &http.Client{Timeout: 3*time.Second}
            response, err := client.Get("http://127.0.0.1:8080/readyz")
            if err != nil { return err }
            defer response.Body.Close()
            if response.StatusCode != 200 { return fmt.Errorf("not ready: %s", response.Status) }
            return nil
        }
        url := os.Getenv("DATABASE_URL")
        if url == "" { return errors.New("DATABASE_URL is required") }
        ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt, syscall.SIGTERM)
        defer stop()
        db, err := store.Open(ctx, url)
        if err != nil { return err }
        defer db.Close()
        startup, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 60*time.Second)
        defer cancel()
        for {
            pingCtx, pingCancel := context.WithTimeout(startup, 2*time.Second)
            err = db.Ping(pingCtx)
            pingCancel()
            if err == nil { break }
            select {
            case <-startup.Done(): return fmt.Errorf("waiting for PostgreSQL: %w", startup.Err())
            case <-time.After(time.Second):
            }
        }
        if err := db.Migrate(startup); err != nil { return fmt.Errorf("database migration: %w", err) }
        address := os.Getenv("ADDR")
        if address == "" { address = ":8080" }
        staticDir := os.Getenv("STATIC_DIR")
        if staticDir == "" { staticDir = "web/dist" }
        server := &http.Server{
            Addr: address, Handler: httpapi.Handler(db, staticDir, logger),
            ReadHeaderTimeout: 5*time.Second, ReadTimeout: 15*time.Second,
            WriteTimeout: 15*time.Second, IdleTimeout: 60*time.Second,
        }
        result := make(chan error, 1)
        go func() { logger.Info("listening", "address", address); result <- server.ListenAndServe() }()
        select {
        case err := <-result:
            if errors.Is(err, http.ErrServerClosed) { return nil }; return err
        case <-ctx.Done():
            shutdown, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 10*time.Second)
            defer cancel()
            return server.Shutdown(shutdown)
        }
    }
    // Package store persists ordered moves. The game package remains the authority
    // for gravity, turns, legal moves, and outcomes.
    package store
    
    import (
        "context"
        "crypto/rand"
        "embed"
        "encoding/hex"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "strings"
    
        "connect4/internal/game"
        "github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
        "github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
    )
    
    //go:embed migrations/*.sql
    var migrations embed.FS
    
    type Postgres struct { pool *pgxpool.Pool }
    
    func Open(ctx context.Context, url string) (*Postgres, error) {
        config, err := pgxpool.ParseConfig(url)
        if err != nil { return nil, err }
        config.MaxConns = 10
        pool, err := pgxpool.NewWithConfig(ctx, config)
        if err != nil { return nil, err }
        return &Postgres{pool: pool}, nil
    }
    
    func (s *Postgres) Close() { s.pool.Close() }
    func (s *Postgres) Ping(ctx context.Context) error { return s.pool.Ping(ctx) }
    
    // Migrate applies versioned SQL files once, in filename order. The advisory lock
    // also makes concurrent application startups safe.
    func (s *Postgres) Migrate(ctx context.Context) error {
        tx, err := s.pool.Begin(ctx)
        if err != nil { return err }
        defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
        if _, err = tx.Exec(ctx, `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(431004)`); err != nil { return err }
        if _, err = tx.Exec(ctx, `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_migrations (
            version text PRIMARY KEY, applied_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now())`); err != nil { return err }
        entries, err := migrations.ReadDir("migrations")
        if err != nil { return err }
        for _, entry := range entries {
            if entry.IsDir() || !strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".sql") { continue }
            var exists bool
            if err = tx.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM schema_migrations WHERE version=$1)`, entry.Name()).Scan(&exists); err != nil { return err }
            if exists { continue }
            sql, err := migrations.ReadFile("migrations/" + entry.Name())
            if err != nil { return err }
            if _, err = tx.Exec(ctx, string(sql)); err != nil { return fmt.Errorf("migration %s: %w", entry.Name(), err) }
            if _, err = tx.Exec(ctx, `INSERT INTO schema_migrations(version) VALUES ($1)`, entry.Name()); err != nil { return err }
        }
        return tx.Commit(ctx)
    }
    
    func (s *Postgres) Create(ctx context.Context) (game.State, error) {
        var bytes [16]byte
        if _, err := rand.Read(bytes[:]); err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        id := hex.EncodeToString(bytes[:])
        if _, err := s.pool.Exec(ctx, `INSERT INTO games(id) VALUES ($1)`, id); err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        return game.New(id), nil
    }
    
    func (s *Postgres) Get(ctx context.Context, id string) (game.State, error) {
        // One query gives a consistent snapshot of the game and all its moves.
        var columns []int
        err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT COALESCE(
            array_agg(m.column_index ORDER BY m.move_number) FILTER (WHERE m.move_number IS NOT NULL),
            '{}'::integer[]) FROM games g LEFT JOIN moves m ON m.game_id=g.id
            WHERE g.id=$1 GROUP BY g.id`, id).Scan(&columns)
        if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) { return game.State{}, game.ErrNotFound }
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        return reconstruct(id, columns)
    }
    
    func (s *Postgres) Move(ctx context.Context, id string, column, expected int) (game.State, error) {
        tx, err := s.pool.BeginTx(ctx, pgx.TxOptions{IsoLevel: pgx.ReadCommitted})
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        defer tx.Rollback(ctx)
    
        // Always lock the parent BEFORE reading moves. A waiting request then sees
        // the previous transaction's committed moves in its next query.
        var lockedID string
        err = tx.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT id FROM games WHERE id=$1 FOR UPDATE`, id).Scan(&lockedID)
        if errors.Is(err, pgx.ErrNoRows) { return game.State{}, game.ErrNotFound }
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        rows, err := tx.Query(ctx, `SELECT column_index FROM moves WHERE game_id=$1 ORDER BY move_number`, id)
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        columns, err := pgx.CollectRows(rows, pgx.RowTo[int])
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        state, err := reconstruct(id, columns)
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        if state.MoveCount != expected { return game.State{}, game.ErrStale }
        if err := state.Drop(column); err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        if _, err = tx.Exec(ctx, `INSERT INTO moves(game_id,move_number,column_index) VALUES ($1,$2,$3)`, id, state.MoveCount, column); err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        if err = tx.Commit(ctx); err != nil { return game.State{}, err }
        return state, nil
    }
    
    func reconstruct(id string, columns []int) (game.State, error) {
        state, err := game.Replay(id, columns)
        // Bad stored history is an internal error, not an invalid browser request.
        if err != nil { return game.State{}, fmt.Errorf("invalid stored history: %v", err) }
        return state, nil
    }

## Activity

    $ cat README.md
    # Connect Four
    
    A local two-player Connect Four game. React renders the board; Go validates every
    move; PostgreSQL stores the ordered history. Both players share one browser on one
    device. Red starts each game.
    
    ## Run locally
    
    Install **Docker Engine with the Compose plugin**, or **Docker Desktop** with WSL
    integration enabled if using Windows/WSL. Docker must be running.
    
    - [Docker Engine for Ubuntu](https://docs.docker.com/engine/install/ubuntu/)
    - [Docker Desktop for Windows / WSL](https://docs.docker.com/desktop/features/wsl/)
    
    From the project directory:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose up --build
    ```
    
    Open **http://localhost:8080**. The image builds the frontend and backend, runs the
    backend unit tests, and starts alongside PostgreSQL. The server waits for the
    database and automatically applies versioned SQL migrations. You do not need Go,
    Node, or PostgreSQL installed on the host for this path.
    
    Click **Start game**, then take turns choosing columns. Filled columns are
    disabled. Connect four discs horizontally, vertically, or diagonally to win. A
    full board without a winner is a draw. The outlined disc is the most recent move.
    **New game** creates a fresh game ID with red starting again.
    
    The browser remembers the active game ID in `localStorage`. Refreshing reloads
    the game from PostgreSQL, including the last-disc highlight and any result.
    PostgreSQL uses a named Docker volume, so ordinary stops and restarts preserve
    games. Clearing browser storage loses the browser's reference to the active game.
    
    Stop containers, preserving games:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose down
    ```
    
    Start in the background and wait for readiness, if Make is installed:
    
    ```bash
    make up
    ```
    
    **Erase all saved games deliberately:** `docker compose down --volumes`.
    
    Port 8080 is bound to the local machine. PostgreSQL is internal to the Compose
    network. The included credentials are for local development.
    
    ## Develop with Go and React
    
    Install [Go 1.23 or newer](https://go.dev/doc/install) (the container uses Go 1.26),
    [Node.js 22 LTS](https://nodejs.org/) (or Node >=20.19), and Make. The Go HTTP server
    uses the standard library; `pgx` supplies PostgreSQL connections. Vite builds and
    serves React during development. No Go framework or ORM is involved.
    
    Start PostgreSQL with its development-only port exposed:
    
    ```bash
    make dev-db
    ```
    
    In a second terminal, install Go dependencies and start the API:
    
    ```bash
    make backend
    ```
    
    In a third terminal, install frontend dependencies and start Vite:
    
    ```bash
    make frontend
    ```
    
    Open **http://localhost:5173**. Vite forwards `/api` requests to Go on port 8080;
    frontend edits update automatically. Restart `make backend` after Go changes.
    The Go server's root page needs a frontend build (`npm --prefix web run build`),
    but its API works independently during development.
    
    Stop the development database with:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose -f compose.yaml -f compose.dev.yaml down
    ```
    
    Environment variables:
    
    | Name | Meaning | Default |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `DATABASE_URL` | PostgreSQL connection string | Required; `make backend` supplies a local value |
    | `ADDR` | Go listen address | `:8080` |
    | `STATIC_DIR` | Built frontend directory | `web/dist` |
    | `TEST_DATABASE_URL` | Dedicated PostgreSQL database for integration tests | Unset; integration tests skip |
    | `BASE_URL` | Running application URL for smoke/browser tests | `http://localhost:8080` |
    
    ## Verify and test
    
    With Node available and the application running, verify startup and an actual
    game against the database:
    
    ```bash
    make verify
    ```
    
    This checks readiness, served HTML, legal moves, a win, retrieval of saved state,
    and rejection of further moves. It creates one small test game in the database.
    
    Run backend unit tests and PostgreSQL integration tests in containers, using a
    separate temporary database that is removed afterward:
    
    ```bash
    make test-container
    ```
    
    For native development, after `go mod download`:
    
    ```bash
    make test
    ```
    
    This runs `go vet`, Go tests with the race detector, and the dependency-free
    JavaScript API tests. PostgreSQL tests skip unless `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set.
    The race detector requires a C compiler when running natively on Linux; the Go
    container includes one. To explicitly require integration tests against your own
    dedicated test database:
    
    ```bash
    TEST_DATABASE_URL='postgres://user:password@localhost:5432/connect4_test?sslmode=disable' make test-integration
    ```
    
    Browser tests require the full application running at `BASE_URL`:
    
    ```bash
    cd web
    npm ci
    npx playwright install --with-deps chromium
    npm run test:e2e
    ```
    
    They cover turn changes, gravity, last-disc highlighting, refresh, new games,
    full columns, wins, draws, stale tabs, a lost response after a committed move,
    keyboard controls, and a small screen. Go tests additionally cover both diagonal
    directions, yellow wins, invalid requests, rejection after game end, a win on the
    42nd move, migration reruns, and simultaneous requests.
    
    ## Kubernetes
    
    Install [kubectl](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/tools/) and
    [kind](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/) alongside Docker. Then:
    
    ```bash
    make k8s-up
    ```
    
    This creates a local `connect4` cluster if necessary, builds and loads the app
    image, applies `deploy/k8s`, waits for readiness, and forwards
    **http://localhost:8080**. Stop Compose first if it already uses that port. Ctrl+C
    stops forwarding; the workloads keep running.
    
    The startup script puts kind's temporary files in a unique directory inside the
    project so Snap-installed Docker can access the image archive. It removes that
    directory after loading, including if loading fails.
    
    There is one app replica and one PostgreSQL replica. A **Deployment** manages the
    app process; a **StatefulSet** gives PostgreSQL a stable identity and a
    **PersistentVolumeClaim** requests disk storage. Restarting pods preserves data.
    Liveness checks detect a stopped process; readiness checks determine whether the
    app can reach PostgreSQL. All helper commands explicitly select the local kind
    context.
    
    Stop the workloads while keeping their data with `make k8s-down`. Running
    `make k8s-up` starts them again. **`make k8s-reset` deletes the local cluster and
    its saved games.** Compose and Kubernetes use separate databases.
    
    The manifests target a local kind cluster, whose default storage provisioner
    supplies the disk. For another cluster, provide a default StorageClass, publish
    the app image to your registry, and update `image` / `imagePullPolicy` in
    `deploy/k8s/app.yaml`. Kubernetes Secrets supply database configuration.
    
    ## How it works
    
    ```text
    Browser (React + remembered game ID)
                     │ HTTP / JSON
                     ▼
    Go server (serves frontend + validates moves)
                     │ SQL
                     ▼
    PostgreSQL (games + ordered moves)
    ```
    
    | Request | Result |
    | --- | --- |
    | `POST /api/games` | `201`: a new game and its random ID |
    | `GET /api/games/{id}` | `200`: reconstructed state, or `404` |
    | `POST /api/games/{id}/moves` | `200`: updated state, or a validation error |
    | `GET /healthz` | Process is responding |
    | `GET /readyz` | Process can reach PostgreSQL |
    
    A move body looks like:
    
    ```json
    { "column": 3, "expectedMoveCount": 8 }
    ```
    
    API columns are numbered **0–6**; the interface labels them **1–7**. Board row zero
    is the top. `board` cells are `0` (empty), `1` (red), or `2` (yellow).
    `status` is `playing`, `won`, or `draw`. `nextPlayer` is zero after completion;
    `winner` is zero unless someone won. `lastMove` is null before the first move,
    otherwise it contains `row`, `column`, and `player`. `availableColumns` is empty
    after completion. A new state includes `id` and `moveCount` as well.
    
    For each move, Go starts a **transaction**: a group of SQL operations that all
    succeed or all roll back. It locks that game's row with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`,
    reads the moves in order, reconstructs the board, compares `expectedMoveCount`,
    checks legality, and appends the new move. Another request for the same game waits
    and then sees the committed history. An outdated request receives
    `409 stale_state`, so a duplicate click cannot silently become the next player's
    turn. The client refreshes the state after a conflict. If a response is lost, it
    requires a reload before allowing another move rather than resending the request.
    
    There is no separate stored board or result to get out of sync with the moves.
    Win detection runs before the full-board draw check, including on move 42.
    
    Main files:
    
    - `internal/game`: pure game rules and their unit tests.
    - `internal/store`: PostgreSQL transactions, migrations, integration tests.
    - `internal/httpapi`: JSON validation, HTTP endpoints, static file serving.
    - `cmd/server`: configuration, startup, and graceful shutdown.
    - `web/src`: React interface, styling, and API client.
    - `web/e2e`: browser tests against the running app.
    - `deploy`: local kind configuration and Kubernetes resources.
    
    ## Troubleshooting
    
    - **Cannot connect to Docker:** start Docker / Docker Desktop. In WSL, enable
      integration for this distribution. Installing the CLI alone does not start an
      engine.
    - **Port already allocated:** stop the other app or change the local port mapping
      in `compose.yaml`; use the matching `BASE_URL` for verification.
    - **kind image loading reports `invalid output path` under `/tmp`:** Snap Docker
      has a different view of temporary directories. The startup script supplies a
      shared project-local `TMPDIR` for kind; rerun `sudo make k8s-up` with the updated
      script. It reuses the existing cluster. See
      [kind's Snap Docker guidance](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/known-issues/#docker-installed-with-snap).
    - **Database not ready:** inspect `docker compose logs db app`. Initial startup
      creates the database and runs migrations automatically.
    - **Cannot download tools, packages, or images:** initial setup requires external
      network access. Offline builds require previously cached dependencies and images.
    - **Reload game message:** the previous move may already be saved. Reload to
      retrieve the authoritative state before continuing.

    DNS resolution errors, no npm dependencies were cached, and administrator access
    was disabled. The JavaScript API tests ran successfully, along with JSX syntax,
    dependency lockfile, YAML, and shell syntax checks.
    
    Docker and Compose were subsequently installed and verified on the host machine.
    The developer confirmed that the game works at http://localhost:8080. Docker
    startup and browser operation are therefore confirmed on the host; this agent
    session still cannot access Docker because of its sandbox restrictions.
    
    The developer also confirmed that `sudo make test-container` passed on the host.
    This runs the Go tests, including the PostgreSQL integration tests, with the race
    detector against a separate temporary database.
    
    The developer confirmed that all seven Playwright browser tests passed on the
    host, covering game interactions, refresh recovery, full columns, wins, draws,
    stale tabs, lost responses, and keyboard play on a small screen.
    
    The developer confirmed successful Kubernetes startup and game persistence
    across replacement of the PostgreSQL and application pods on the host. This
    completes the planned first-iteration verification: Docker startup, backend and
    PostgreSQL tests, all seven browser tests, and Kubernetes startup and persistence.
    
    `node scripts/verify.mjs` remains available as a separate smoke check against a
    running application; successful execution of that standalone script has not been
    reported.

    # Connect Four
    
    A two-player game for one device, built with React, Go, and PostgreSQL.
    
    ## Run locally
    
    Prerequisite: Docker with Compose installed and running. From the project directory:
    
    ```bash
    docker compose up --build
    ```
    
    Open **http://localhost:8080**. The build includes the frontend and backend; the
    server applies database migrations automatically. No host Go or PostgreSQL
    installation is needed.
    
    Stop with `docker compose down`; saved games stay in the database volume. The
    browser remembers the current game ID, so refreshing restores it. Clearing
    browser storage loses that reference.
    
    Make is required for the helper commands below. If Docker requires `sudo`, use it
    for Docker commands and Make targets that invoke Docker. The supplied database
    credentials are for local development.
    
    ## Tests
    
    Run the backend and PostgreSQL tests with race detection in containers:
    
    ```bash
    make test-container
    ```
    
    This uses a separate temporary database and removes it afterward.
    
    For frontend and browser checks, install Node.js 20.19+ (20.x) or 22.12+, and npm.
    Keep the app running at port 8080 and use another terminal in the project directory:
    
    ```bash
    npm --prefix web ci
    npm --prefix web test
    (cd web && npx playwright install --with-deps chromium)
    make e2e
    make verify
    ```
    
    `make e2e` runs seven browser tests covering play, refresh, new games, outcomes,
    stale tabs, lost responses, and keyboard play on a small screen. Chromium
    installation is needed for browser tests. `make verify` is a smoke check of the
    running app and API; it creates one test game. Set `BASE_URL` for a different URL.
    
    The app image build also runs `go vet`, Go unit tests with race detection,
    JavaScript API tests, and the frontend production build.
    
    ## Kubernetes
    
    Prerequisites: Docker, Make, [kubectl](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/tools/),
    and [kind](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/).
    
    ```bash
    docker compose down
    make k8s-up
    ```
    
    This creates or reuses the local cluster, builds and loads the app, waits for
    readiness, and forwards **http://localhost:8080**. Leave that terminal open.
    Ctrl+C stops forwarding; the workloads continue running.
    
    There is one app replica and one PostgreSQL replica with persistent storage.
    Compose and Kubernetes use separate databases. `make k8s-down` stops the
    Kubernetes workloads while keeping their data. Keep using the same user for
    Kubernetes commands, including `sudo` if you started the cluster with it.
    
    ## Decisions and tradeoffs
    
    Go serves the API and built React files, keeping deployment to one app container
    plus PostgreSQL. The board and result are rebuilt from at most 42 stored moves,
    avoiding a second copy of game state. Transactions, a lock on the game row, and
    an expected move count prevent conflicting or stale requests from adding moves.
    
    This iteration focuses on two players sharing a browser. The browser stores the
    game ID, and PostgreSQL runs as a single instance without database failover.
    With more time, the next steps would be backup/restore tooling and automated
    Kubernetes recovery checks.
    
    ## Verified
    
    Confirmed on the host: Docker startup and browser play, backend/PostgreSQL tests
    with race detection, all seven browser tests, and Kubernetes startup with game
    persistence across replacement of the app and database pods.
    
    The browser suite passed before the recent UI edits. Subsequent frontend builds
    and JavaScript API tests passed; the updated browser suite has not been rerun.
    A successful run of the standalone `make verify` smoke check has not been reported.
    ✓ • 124ms
